  ## 2. What the project actually is (one paragraph)

  Order Get It Right — Truth as a Service, v1.0.0. A deterministic business audit and valuation program. Pure Python
  3.12+, no network in the runtime, no LLM in the audit path. Ingests business documents (.txt, .docx, .pdf), extracts
  structured evidence, runs a 4-gate deterministic pipeline (54-pattern Deception ontology v3.9 + BBFB engine + 2-stage
  real-options lattice + Decision gate), generates Markdown / PDF / DOCX reports, drafts Section 56 ACL demand letters
  and Section 177 Evidence Act affidavits, seals every action to a SHA-256 Merkle chain. Operator: Justin Barnett, South
  Australia. Jurisdiction: Commonwealth of Australia / ACL / Evidence Act 1995. Six non-negotiables: determinism, no
  black boxes, truth ledger, 10% tau firewall, portable, human-documentable.

  ———

  ### C. The 4-gate pipeline (what audit_cli runs)

  .txt / .docx / .pdf
     │
     ▼
  [Gate 1] Deception scanner (54-pattern ontology v3.9 + Shannon entropy)
     │   DD-001..DD-054 patterns, deception score 0..1, verdict
     ▼
  [Gate 2] BBFB engine (LAW multiplicative veto + GRACE quadratic penalty + FRUIT weighted product + CVS)
     │   Performance ≥ 0.50, Efficiency ≥ 0.30, Warranty ≥ 1.00, Issue density ≤ 0.10, Violation ratio ≤ 0.05
     ▼
  [Gate 3] Real-options lattice (2-stage compound binomial CRR)
     │   S0=55, K1=18, K2=10, T1=3, T2=3, r=0.05, σ1=0.30, σ2=0.20
     ▼
  [Gate 4] Decision (GO / DEFER / TEST FIRST / REJECT)
     │   + Tau firewall (10% extraction ceiling) + Squeal Protocol (CRITICAL pattern → squeal file)
     ▼
  output: Markdown + PDF + DOCX report + Section 56 ACL demand letter + Section 177 Evidence Act affidavit
  seal:   one block on the Merkle chain (SHA-256 over prev_hash + payload + timestamp)

  ### D. The 6 non-negotiables

  1. Determinism — same input + same config = same output, on any host.
  2. No black boxes — every formula in 01_Methodology\. Every number in the UI has a Python function behind it. See
     04_Validation\AUDIT_NO_BLACK_BOX.md.

  3. Truth ledger — every action sealed to a SHA-256 Merkle chain.
  4. Tau firewall — 10% extraction ceiling is enforced.
  5. Portable — Python install + Tauri binary, both run the same engine.
  6. Human-documentable — every change and incident recorded with the binId of the exact binary that was running.

• # Yes. I read them all. Here is the honest analysis.

  These 11 files are not "audit" files in the sense of the OrderGetItRight build. They are a separate project from a
  different era — the "Sovereign Node 9010 / Project ValueForge / Groknett" project that was developed in May 2026
  against a Linux/WSL2/Docker/Azure target. I did not read them in the first pass because I was focused on the build's
  own validation documents. Now I have, and what they show is exactly what I told you in my first reply: the "mess" in
  the parent folder is the noise of a different, abandoned project lineage, not damage to your current build.

  Below is what I found, what it has to do with OrderGetItRight, and what to do with it.

  ## 2. What they actually say, and what it means for OrderGetItRight

  There is one really important thing these files do, and I am going to be very direct about it.

  ### 2.1 The Sovereign Node 9010 project is the predecessor of OrderGetItRight

  Reading these files in order, the lineage is unmistakable:

  1. March 2026 (_drift_production.txt, _drift_framework.txt, _drift_requirements.txt): the project is called Sovereign
     Node 9010 / Project ValueForge / Groknett. It is on a 65% / 35% gap footing. The folder layout is 00-99
     (00_Strategy, 01_Methodology, 02_Execution, 03_Data_Proxies, 04_Validation — i.e. the same 5-folder spine your
     current project has). The constants are the same: TAU = 0.10, BBFB with LAW/GRACE/FRUIT, ISO Date Mandate, Merkle
     chain, ACL/NSW Evidence Act s 177. The target is Azure + WSL2 + Docker + a TypeScript/Node stack.

  2. May 2026 (_drift_transition.txt — the 5,096-line Grok transcript): the operator migrated the Sovereign Node 9010
     codebase to Grok (xAI), who expanded the deception ontology from 8 patterns → 20 → 30 → 40. The Grok conversation
     ends with a "Sovereign-Node-9010-v3.6-Final-Release.tar.gz" declared production-ready at 57 KB, with 40 patterns,
     async+concurrent processing, CSV/JSON export, and a "100% deterministic" claim.

  3. May 31 2026 (_drift_consolidated.txt, _drift_blueprint.txt, _drift_zerotouch.txt, _review_extra_docs.txt): the
     operator had a change of direction. The new target is LDDE-ZeroTouch — local-first, zero-cloud, MSI Prestige 16 +
     external SSD, Qwen2.5-coder:7b-instruct-q4_K_M via Ollama, OpenHands orchestrator, PTCF prompting, Jesus Code
     verifier. The Sovereign Node stuff is explicitly told to be stripped: > Strip all sovereignnode9010 governance math
     and unproven concepts. Keep only proven, hardware-specific, executable patterns. The new build is for a local LLM
     coding assistant, not a business audit engine.

  4. 2026-07-11 to 2026-07-16 (the OrderGetItRight build, sealed to the Merkle chain at 02_Technical/03_Vault/
     facts_registry.json): the operator (with the help of various AI agents) re-founded the project as Order Get It
     Right — Truth as a Service. Same 00-99 spatial hierarchy. Same BBFB engine. Same 10% tau. Same Merkle chain. Same
     ACL + Evidence Act 1995 jurisdiction. Same operator: Justin Barnett. But the target is now: pure Python 3.12+
     stdlib, no network, no LLM in the audit path, Tauri desktop binary, hard-copy backup plan with paper card.

  The "drift" is the gap between the Sovereign Node 9010 / LDDE-ZeroTouch era and the OrderGetItRight era. These
  _drift_* and _review_* files are the work journal of the transition — they were dropped in the parent folder when the
  operator (or an agent) was reading the old Sovereign Node docs to extract the parts that were still useful (the BBFB
  math, the deception ontology, the 00-99 hierarchy, the operator name, the jurisdiction) and re-founding the project on
  a deterministic, air-gapped foundation.

  ### 2.4 The two pieces that look like drift but are actually intentional

  1. The folder structure is 02_Execution / 02_Technical / 02_Tech etc. — the Sovereign Node doc calls the technical
     folder 02_Execution; the current build calls it 02_Technical. This is a deliberate rename, not drift. The
     04_Validation/RESEARCH_COMPATIBILITY_2026-07-12.md doc (sealed at block 889) records this.

  2. The chain in OrderGetItRight is JSON, not SQLite. Sovereign Node 9010 used sql.js (refactored to bypass native
     compilation on MSI). OrderGetItRight uses a flat JSON file with a deterministic append-only Merkle chain. This is a
     deliberate architectural simplification — JSON is human-readable, git-diffable, and does not need a SQL engine. The
     chain still verifies byte-for-byte.

  ———

  ## 3. The honest answer to "are these audit files?"

  No. They are not part of the OrderGetItRight audit trail. The audit trail for the current project is:

  - 04_Validation/changelog.log (30 lines, the human record)
  - 02_Technical/03_Vault/facts_registry.json (3,033 blocks, the Merkle chain)
  - 04_Validation/RECONCILIATION_2026-07-16.md (the gap explanation)
  - 04_Validation/CONTEXT_WINDOW.md (the orientation packet)
  - 04_Validation/MAINTENANCE_PLAN.txt (the maintenance contract)
  - 04_Validation/HANDOVER_TO_AUDITOR.md (the auditor's verification script)
  - 04_Validation/AUDIT_NO_BLACK_BOX.md and 04_Validation/AUDIT_NO_NETWORK.md (the two-pillar claims with file:line
    traceability)

  - 04_Validation/STAGE_PAPER_{DAILY,WEEKLY,MONTHLY,QUARTERLY,ANNUAL}.txt (5 operator runbooks)
  - 04_Validation/HANDOVER_TO_NEW_OPERATOR.md (the successor's orientation)
  - 04_Validation/INTELLECTUAL_PROPERTY_RIGHTS.txt (the legal rights reservation)
  - 04_Validation/YELLOW_RIBBON.md (the welcome mat)
  - 04_Validation/scripts/phase_4_refresh_fingerprints.py (the 6-fingerprints re-derivation script)
  - 04_Validation/scripts/audit_no_network.py (the no-network audit script)
  - 04_Validation/scripts/verify_chain.py (the chain verification script)

  The _drift_* and _review_* files are the work journal of the prior project (Sovereign Node 9010 / Project ValueForge /
  Groknett / LDDE-ZeroTouch), kept in the parent folder because the operator (or an agent) was using them as reference
  material when re-founding the project. They are not part of the audit trail because the audit trail is the chain +
  changelog + the 14 docs in 04_Validation/, not these reference docs.

  The fact that these files survived into the current project folder without being deleted is normal — they are how a
  careful operator keeps the receipts of the prior project so the lineage is auditable. They should not be deleted. They
  should be moved to OrderGetItRight/99_Archive/parent-folder-noise-2026-07-16/ so the working directory is clean, but
  kept on disk for the same reason the .bak-pre-* files in the project are kept — they are the receipts of the work that
  led here.

  ———

  ## 4. What I was wrong about in my first reply

  In my first reply, I listed the 8 _drift_*.txt files and 2 _review_*.txt files in the "noise" table and said "scratch
  files from various AI agents (Claude, Codex, Onyx, Gemini)". That was partially right and partially wrong.

  Right: they are not part of the OrderGetItRight build. They do not get sealed to the chain. They do not affect the
  chain's verification.

  Wrong: I called them "scratch files from various AI agents." They are not scratch. They are the deliverables of a
  prior project (Sovereign Node 9010 / Project ValueForge, May 2026) and a transitional project (LDDE-ZeroTouch, late
  May 2026) that the same operator (Justin Barnett) ran before re-founding the current project. They were generated by
  Grok (xAI), the operator, and other agents working on the prior project. They were left in the parent folder when the
  operator moved to OrderGetItRight. They are the lineage, not the noise.

  A more honest description would have been: "These 11 files are the work journal of the prior project (Sovereign Node
  9010 / Project ValueForge, May 2026) and the transitional project (LDDE-ZeroTouch, late May 2026). They document the
  5-folder 00-99 hierarchy, the BBFB engine, the 10% Tau firewall, and the 30→40-pattern deception ontology that
  OrderGetItRight inherited and extended. The current project kept the parts that worked (the math, the hierarchy, the
  ontology names, the operator name, the jurisdiction) and dropped the parts that did not fit the new design (WSL2/
  Docker/Azure deployment, the LLM-in-the-audit-loop, the TypeScript/sql.js stack, the User Exhaustion middleware)."

  I should have said that the first time. I am saying it now.

  ———

  ## 5. What to do with them

  Three options, in order of recommendation:

  1. Recommended: move them to 99_Archive/parent-folder-noise-2026-07-16/. This keeps the receipts on disk, in the
     project tree, in the frozen-archive folder that is already documented in the build. The chain's fingerprint (REF-4:
     tree shape) will change, but REF-1, REF-2, REF-3, REF-5 will not. Run python 04_Validation/scripts/
     phase_4_refresh_fingerprints.py to re-derive and refresh YELLOW_RIBBON.md + QUICK_REFERENCE_CARD.txt. This is a
     paperwork change; the chain itself does not need a new seal. The cost is one terminal command.

  2. Alternative: leave them where they are. They are not breaking anything. The chain still verifies. The build still
     runs. The parent folder is messy but the project folder is clean. This is fine for now.

  3. Strongly NOT recommended: delete them. They are the only on-disk record of the prior project's evolution. If a
     third party ever asks "where did the BBFB engine come from" or "why is the operator's name Justin Barnett in 7
     places", these are the receipts. Delete only if you have a separate cold backup (the SDXC at D:\OrderGetItRight\ or
     the 1-2-3 paper backup plan) that contains them.

  One thing to add to the chain as a single observation seal, if you want full hygiene:

  A LINEAGE_RECEIPT_2026_07_16 block, type observation, that says: "The 11 files in the parent folder (_drift_*.txt,
  _review_*.txt, _drift_*.docx, etc.) are the work journal of the predecessor project Sovereign Node 9010 / Project
  ValueForge (May 2026) and the transitional project LDDE-ZeroTouch (late May 2026). They document the 00-99 spatial
  hierarchy, the BBFB engine, the 10% Tau firewall, and the 30→40-pattern deception ontology that this project inherited
  and extended. They are not part of this project's chain. They are kept on disk as lineage receipts. Recommended next
  step: move to 99_Archive/parent-folder-noise-2026-07-16/ and refresh REF-4 + the paper card."

  This is paperwork, not state change. The chain tip will not change (3,033 blocks → 3,034). I can write and seal it in
  one command if you want. Or I can move the files to 99_Archive\ and refresh the fingerprints without adding a new
  block. Tell me which.

  ———

  ## 6. The single most important sentence

  OrderGetItRight is the same operator, the same BBFB math, the same 00-99 hierarchy, the same 10% Tau firewall, the
  same 54-pattern deception ontology (with 14 more than the prior project), the same Merkle chain, the same ACL +
  Evidence Act 1995 jurisdiction, the same MSI Prestige 16 laptop, the same human-in-the-loop maintenance discipline —
  but rebuilt on a different foundation: pure Windows Python 3.12+ stdlib, no network, no LLM in the audit loop, Tauri
  desktop binary, hard-copy backup plan with paper card.

  The prior project was a concept (Sovereign Node 9010 v3.6 "production-ready" with 40 patterns, 65% operational, Azure
  + WSL2 + Docker target). The current project is operational (3,033 blocks, 50/51 tests green, 0 network imports, 6 re-
  derivable fingerprints, Tauri binary built, hard-copy backup plan in place). The drift files are the receipt that the
  work moved forward, not the receipt that the work broke.

  # Order Get It Right — Whole-Project Report

  Path: C:\Users\justo\OneDrive\Documents\My Project\OrderGetItRight\
  Version: 1.0.0  •  Build date: 2026-07-12  •  Operator: Justin Barnett  •  Jurisdiction: Commonwealth of Australia
  Runtime: Python 3.14.6, pure stdlib, no LLM in the audit path, air-gapped
  Tauri binary: built (4,847,104 bytes, 2026-07-12)

  ———

  ## 1. What it is, in one paragraph

  A deterministic business audit and valuation engine. You hand it any business document (warranty response, contract
  clause, invoice, supplier letter, court filing). It runs a 4-gate deterministic pipeline — 54-pattern deception
  ontology v3.9 with Shannon entropy, BBFB engine (LAW multiplicative veto + GRACE quadratic penalty + FRUIT weighted
  product), two-stage compound binomial real-options lattice, decision gate — then seals the verdict to a SHA-256 Merkle
  chain and emits Markdown / PDF / DOCX reports plus a Section 177 Evidence Act affidavit and a Section 56 ACL demand
  letter. Every action is on the chain. Every chain block re-derives. Every output number is traceable to a file:line in
  the source. There is no model server, no cloud API, no probabilistic black box in the audit path. A third party can
  take the project folder, run python -m src.verify_chain on a clean Windows host, and confirm the Merkle root matches
  the printed paper card in 30 seconds. The build is operational, not finished — it is meant to be maintained, not
  shipped.

  ———

  The 8 root-level *_SEAL.py files are build-session receipts — each one is a one-shot script that sealed a specific
  block to the chain (A5 deploy dry-run, C1-C5 doc maintenance, D1 USB plan, D5 agent wiring, deploy hardening, model
  swap revert, ontology canonical JSON, research quick-wins). They are part of the receipts; the chain references the
  exact hashes they wrote. They are not active code.

  from 03_Vault/ or 04_Validation/; the only legal interface to the vault is src/io/vault_io.py.

  #### 3.4.3 02_Technical/src/agents/ — 11 files

   File                        Role
  ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━  ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   orchestrator.py             The single runtime entry point. Orchestrator.process_input(category, statement,
                               product_evidence) runs the 5-step hand-off: Form_Entry_Agent → Audit_Review_Agent →
                               Lattice_Compute_Agent → Ledger_Seal_Agent → Decision. Each step creates a job token (URN
                               format OGIR:<SPACE>:<ACTION>), claims it, runs the work, closes it. Returns
                               {finalAction, reason, deceptionGate, valuationGate, bbfbGate, ledgerRoot, tau}. The
                               decision is REFUSED on CRITICAL patterns, REVIEW_REQUIRED on HIGH, REJECT on BBFB non-
                               compliance, TEST FIRST on lattice DEFER, GO otherwise. The orchestrator's __init__ does
                               not seed the facts registry — that foot-gun was removed at block 1392 (2026-07-12
                               03:40:36Z).
  ──────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   tau_firewall.py             10% extraction ceiling enforcement. TauFirewall measures audit-runtime vs wall-clock,
                               anchored to the first measurement (not process start, so cold-start I/O doesn't count).
                               Raises StructuralRefusal when ratio > 0.10. Thread-safe with RLock. The
                               assert_within_ceiling(label) method is the gate every heavy operation passes through.
  ──────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   job_delegator.py            MCP Handshake of Delegation. AgentJobDelegator creates SHA-256-of-canonical-JSON job
                               tokens, supports create/claim/close/refuse, persists to 03_Vault/job_registry.json
                               (Merkle-chained separately from facts). URN format OGIR:<SPACE>:<ACTION>; SPATIAL_MAP =
                               {00 STRATEGY, 01 METHODOLOGY, 02 TECHNICAL, 03 VAULT, 04 VALIDATION, 99 ARCHIVE}. Valid
                               statuses QUEUED/IN_PROGRESS/COMPLETED/REFUSED/FAILED. Every job is sealed to the facts
                               chain.
  ──────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   form_entry_agent.py         The intake role. draft_fact(category, statement, source) returns a DraftFact with URN
                               OGIR:02:FORM_ENTRY. research(query) walks 00_Strategy / 01_Methodology / 02_Technical /
                               docs for files matching all the search terms (case-insensitive, max 200 hits).
                               normalize_real_world_claim(raw_text) is a deterministic regex-only parser that extracts
                               productName, pricePaid, priceAdvertised, specClaimed, specClaimedUnit, specMeasured,
                               warrantyMonths, monthsToFailure, knownIssues, totalFeaturesOrParts,
                               regulatoryRequirements, violationsFound from free text.
  ──────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   audit_review_agent.py       The test + contradiction-hunting role. audit(text, context) runs
                               deception_scanner.audit_text, returns a 3-state verdict: SUPPRESSED (any CRITICAL
                               pattern), FLAGGED (any HIGH), CLEAN. hunt(statement) walks 02_Technical / deploy / docs
                               for code that contradicts the operator's statement (e.g. urllib, random., time.time()
                               etc.).
  ──────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   lattice_compute_agent.py    The real-world business test role. compute_bbfb(evidence) runs the BBFB engine + a real-
                               options lattice (with default deception_score=0, entropy=0), returns a BusinessVerdict
                               with bbfb_compliant, bbfb_cvs, bbfb_grace_risk, valuation_decision, valuation_total,
                               summary.
  ──────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   ledger_seal_agent.py        The audit log + Merkle chain reviewer. seal_fact(event_type, payload) calls
                               vault_io.append_block. verify_root() re-derives the chain from disk and compares to the
                               registry's claimed root, returning {claimed_root, recomputed_root, matches, block_count,
                               broken_at}.
  ──────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   affidavit_agent.py          The overseer. compile_affidavit() reads the entire chain and produces a Section 177 /
                               Section 79 certificate with the operator's signature line. compare_to_spec() walks
                               02_Technical/src/ for hardcoded values that disagree with the constants (e.g. a literal
                               = 0.10 in code would be flagged as a spec disagreement).
  ──────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   monitor_agent.py            The human-in-the-loop oversight role. run_briefing() re-derives the chain root from
                               disk, scans the vault + logs + Squeal + changelog for "hide-pattern" markers
                               (delete_block, edit_block, rewrite_chain, tamper, self_pardon, backdoor keywords),
                               cross-checks SUPPRESSED verdicts against REFUSAL/Squeal records, returns a structured
                               Incident Briefing as Markdown. The operator reads, signs, seals the signed briefing.
                               This is the role that catches a third party (or a runaway model) editing a verdict,
                               hiding a block, or adding a fake fact.
  ──────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   inventory_agent.py          The drive-inventory agent (v1.0.0, 26 KB). Walks local paths the operator names,
                               classifies every file into PROJECT_CODE / PROJECT_DOC / BUILD_ARTEFACT / PROJECT_INPUT /
                               LIBRARY_NOISE / PERSONAL / DUPLICATE / REDACTED_LOCATION / UNKNOWN, hashes via streaming
                               SHA-256, redacts credential-shaped filenames from the chain entirely, emits a
                               deterministic Markdown report. Library noise signatures (virtualenv, pytest, pip) are
                               caught by content-sniff. First run on 2026-07-11 sealed 5,495 files across 4 default
                               roots (C:\Users\justo.claude\OrderGetItRight, OneDrive\Documents\to the spoils go,
                               OneDrive\Documents\gemini to evalue, C:\Users\justo\Downloads) in 2.3 s. The report is
                               at data/outbox/DRIVE_INVENTORY_20260711T221839Z.md (100 KB) and data/outbox/
                               DRIVE_INVENTORY_20260711T221839Z.records.json (3.1 MB).
  ──────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   tau_firewall.py             (already described)
  ──────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   __init__.py                 1-line docstring.

  #### 3.4.4 02_Technical/src/engines/ — 10 files

   File                What it does
  ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━  ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   deception_scanne    The 52-pattern scanner. shannon_entropy(text) is character-level, returns {shannonEntropy,
   r.py                normalizedEntropy, characterDistribution, anomalyFlag (>4.5), lowEntropyFlag (<2.5)}.
                       detect_patterns_with_confidence(text, prioritized) matches every pattern whose indicators appear
                       (case-insensitive substring), sorts by confidence desc, with a repetition-hammering guard that
                       catches "I apologize. I apologize. I apologize." and adds it to DD-036.
                       calculate_deception_probability(patterns, anomaly) is 0.6·avg_conf + 0.2·coverage + 0.2·anomaly.
                       audit_text() is the top-level orchestrator: runs entropy + patterns + probability, builds
                       forensic reasoning, triggers Squeal if structuralDeceptionFlag = (probability > 0.75) or any
                       CRITICAL pattern.
  ──────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   deception_ontolo    The 54-pattern dataset (DD-001 to DD-054), each as a Pydantic DeceptionPattern with id, name,
   gy_data.py          category, description, indicators (list of substrings), severity, threshold.
  ──────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   bbfb_engine.py      The BBFB pipeline. calculate_bbfb(evidence) computes 5 ratios (price, spec, warranty, issue,
                       violation), runs 6 LAW gates (priceFairness ≥ 1.0, specAccuracy ≥ 0.50, efficiencyAdequacy ≥
                       0.30, warrantyAdequacy ≥ 1.00, issueDensity ≥ 0.90 of inverted ratio, complianceClean ≥ 0.95 of
                       inverted ratio), then GRACE penalty 2.0·(p_fail² + d_tech² + g_comp²), then FRUIT weighted
                       product, then CVS = LAW·(FRUIT - Penalty_GRACE). Returns BBFBResult with all 6 LAW gates, GRACE,
                       FRUIT, and overallCompliant. The multiplicative veto means a single failing gate forces the
                       whole product to 0.
  ──────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   real_options_lat    The Cox-Ross-Rubinstein compound binomial. _binomial_lattice(S0, K, T, r, σ, n) does back-
   tice.py             induction on a CRR tree. hardened_compound_binomial_gate(deception_score, entropy) adjusts σ₁ =
                       clamp(σ₁·(1+deception·0.35), 0.05, 0.95), σ₂ = clamp(σ₂·(1+entropy/9.0), 0.05, 0.90), computes
                       V₁, applies learning_delta = 10·(1 - 0.6·deception), computes V₂ from V₁+learning_delta as new
                       S0, total = V₁+V₂, threshold = 0.85·(K1+K2), decision = GO if total > threshold else DEFER.
  ──────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   facts_registry.p    The in-memory fact store with Pydantic validation. Valid categories: Technical / Governance /
   y                   Forensic. add_fact raises on duplicate (statement, source), verify_fact marks verified and
                       seals, delete_fact seals, get_stats returns the in-memory counts plus the vault Merkle stats.
                       Reset and seed are owned by _seed_facts_once() in app.py, not by the registry itself.
  ──────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   acl_demand_gener    The Section 56 ACL demand letter generator. generate_acl_demand(invoice_spec, hardware_id,
   ator.py             deception_report, consumer, supplier, bbfb_score) returns a Markdown document with parties, the
                       material breach (invoice vs hardware), full forensic evidence (entropy, deception patterns,
                       probability, BBFB if provided), the demand (full refund in 14 business days OR replacement OR
                       cost of correction), consequences of non-compliance (ACCC + State/Territory Consumer Affairs +
                       legal proceedings), and the operator's signature block.
  ──────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   legal_affidavit_    The Section 177 / Section 79 NSW Evidence Act expert certificate generator.
   generator.py        compile_full_affidavit() walks every block in the chain, formats each as BLOCK INDEX /
                       TIMESTAMP / EVENT CLASSIFICATION / CRYPTOGRAPHIC SEAL (SHA-256) / PREVIOUS BLOCK LINK / FORENSIC
                       NIZK PROOF / FACTUAL CONTENT, adds a verification statement, and an operator signature line.
                       save_to_vault() writes it to the vault directory.
  ──────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   squeal_protocol.    The high-deception audit trail. write_squeal_report(report, session_id, trigger) writes a JSON
   py                  file to 04_Validation/squeal-reports/ with timestamp, snippet, probability, critical patterns,
                       forensic summary, and a recommendation (ESCALATE_TO_LEGAL for any CRITICAL, MONITOR_CLOSELY
                       otherwise).
  ──────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   evaluation_cases    8 hand-curated EvaluationCase objects (EVAL-001..EVAL-008): corporate evasion, AI safety
   .py                 hedging, bureaucratic redirection, scope creep, false certainty, kelvanistic baseline (factual),
                       direct user correction, technical specification. Each has expectedDeceptive,
                       expectedMinPatterns, expectedMaxDeceptionProbability, tags.
  ──────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   evaluation_servi    run_evaluation_suite(cases, suite_name) returns accuracy/precision/recall/F1 and per-case
   ce.py               results. Deceptive threshold = 0.3.
  ──────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   __init__.py         1-line docstring.

  #### 3.4.5 02_Technical/src/io/ — 6 files

   File                  What it does
  ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━  ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

                         Merkle hash chain logic lives here, NOT in a separate vault module, because the boundary rule
                         says code in 02_Technical cannot import from 03_Vault. append_block(event_type, payload) reads
                         the registry, computes current_hash = SHA256(previous_hash + canonical_json({event, payload,
                         ts})), builds a NIZK placeholder `SHA256(canonical_json(payload) + "
  ────────────────────  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   pipeline.py           The end-to-end batch document audit. process_file(input_path, outbox_dir, formats) extracts
                         text, runs deception_scanner.audit_text, parses ProductEvidence if the text contains product-
                         like terms, runs bbfb_engine.calculate_bbfb, generates an ACL demand if structuralDeception
                         AND (BBFB non-compliant OR no BBFB and prob > 0.75), writes Markdown + PDF + DOCX.
                         process_directory(inbox, outbox, formats) walks a directory with a global EJECT_REQUESTED flag
                         for early termination.
  ────────────────────  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   evidence_parser.py    The extract_product_evidence(text) regex-only parser. Returns ParsedEvidence with confidence
                         (low / medium / high based on hit count). Spec values are classified as claimed vs measured by
                         the closest preceding keyword (rated, claimed, advertised vs measured, actual, tested,
                         observed).
  ────────────────────  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   report_writer.py      The Markdown / PDF / DOCX writer. build_markdown produces the human-readable forensic report.
                         write_pdf uses reportlab. write_docx uses python-docx. write_report(report, out_dir,
                         base_name, formats) returns the list of output paths.
  ────────────────────  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   extractors.py         extract_txt (utf-8 with latin-1 fallback), extract_docx (paragraphs + table cells via python-
                         docx), extract_pdf (pypdf, exception-tolerant per page). SUPPORTED_INPUTS = {".txt", ".docx",
                         ".pdf"}.

   __init__.py           1-line docstring.

   File                     What it is

   agentic_repl.py          The Ollama-backed agentic REPL. Spawns a local LLM with 9 tool functions, loops on
                            tool_calls. The LLM is tcoxav/aegis:latest (1.5B Qwen2, tool-capable; the alternative
                            dolphin-mistral does not support tools). Uses stdlib urllib.request (no ollama package).
                            Tools: audit_text, evaluate_suite, verify_chain, list_facts, add_fact, seal_custom_event,

                            http://127.0.0.1:3000 — it does NOT import from src/. This is the OpenClaw pattern: a local

  ───────────────────────  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   agentic_repl_tools.py    The 9 tool function definitions, each with Google-style Args/Returns docstrings that become

                            by hand (no inspect.signature magic, no ollama Python package).
  ───────────────────────  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   discovery_agent.py       The forward-discovery agent. Walks a target environment before the build enters it. 14
                            sections: HOST, OS, Python, CPU, MEMORY, DISK, INSTALLED PACKAGES, LISTENING PORTS, DNS
                            RESOLUTION, NETWORK POSTURE, BUILD FOOTPRINT, LOCKFILES, THIRD-PARTY VENDOR RISK, OPERATOR
                            FOOTPRINT. Returns a Deterministic Discovery Report with a SHA-256 fingerprint. Same target
                            + same fingerprint = same report byte-for-byte.

  These three are the operator CLI surface — they live outside the no-network audit scope. The no-network audit only
  scans 02_Technical/src/; these are intentionally out of scope because they use urllib.request to talk to Ollama + the
  FastAPI server.

  #### 3.4.10 02_Technical/web/ — 1 file

  index.html is a single-file dark-themed web UI, 474 lines, 10 tabs: Dashboard, Deception Scanner, BBFB Engine, Real-
  Options Lattice, Facts Registry, Merkle Truth Ledger, Evaluation Suite, Batch Audit, Legal Affidavit, Changelog. Auto-
  detects Tauri vs browser mode. In Tauri mode, calls window.__TAURI__.core.invoke('get_startup_info') and logs every
  command via log_command. In browser mode, falls back to fetch on the same FastAPI server. No external resources, no
  CDN, no fonts from the internet.

  ## 4. The six non-negotiables (how the build actually lives them)

   #      Non-negotiable               How it is enforced
  ━━━━━  ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━  ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
   1      Absolute determinism         The orchestrator determinism test
                                       (test_orchestrator_does_not_wipe_operator_facts,
                                       test_evaluation_suite_is_deterministic — 3 consecutive runs produce byte-
                                       identical output), the chain re-derivation, the verify_chain.py standalone
                                       script, the _canonical_json helper, the dataclasses(frozen=True, ...) patterns,
                                       the 19 named constants in config/constants.py.
  ─────  ───────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   2      No black boxes               AUDIT_NO_BLACK_BOX.md lists 14 pillars of audit output with file:line
                                       references. test_no_black_box_audit.py (or rather, the same audit_no_network.py
                                       AST scan + the AUDIT_NO_BLACK_BOX.md table) verifies. The build does NOT use any
                                       LLM, model server, embedding, vector DB, or closed-source library in the request
                                       path. The third-party LLM in the agentic REPL is a tool the operator uses; it is
                                       NOT in the audit decision loop.
  ─────  ───────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   3      Truth ledger                 02_Technical/03_Vault/facts_registry.json, 3,102 blocks, root
                                       7c348a24d6fd6c3395aee9b7eba2ab89b15e49ec98bd255bedc11f6dfe160504. Every state
                                       change since 2026-07-11T17:15:10Z is a sealed block. The chain re-derives on any
                                       host in 30 seconds. The chain matches the printed paper card.
  ─────  ───────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   4      Tau firewall                 tau_firewall.py, ceiling 0.10, enforced by
                                       TauFirewall.assert_within_ceiling(label) in every heavy operation. The constant
                                       is TAU_EXTRACTION_CEILING = 0.10 in config/constants.py.
  ─────  ───────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   5      Portable deployment          deploy/deploy.ps1 provisions a Windows host in one command. deploy/build-
                                       tauri.ps1 builds the Tauri desktop binary. The Tauri binary is built (4.85 MB)
                                       and the MSI + NSIS installers are present. The 1-2-3 backup plan covers the
                                       recovery story.
  ─────  ───────────────────────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   6      Human-documentable change    04_Validation/changelog.log is a JSONL file, 50 lines, one entry per cycle. The
                                       chain is the audit trail. The IP-rights document grants the auditor the right to
                                       verify (clause c), copy the backup plan (clause d), and extend the build with
                                       new agents (clause e).

  ## 7. The 00-99 spatial hierarchy (the project structure as a contract)

  00  Strategy & Governance   ← STRATEGY.md, GOVERNANCE.md (the contract)
  01  Methodology             ← DECEPTION_ONTOLOGY.md, MATHEMATICS.md, REAL_OPTIONS_LATTICE.md (the math)
  02  Technical               ← src/, config/, tools/, tauri-shell/, web/, 03_Vault/, 04_Validation/ (the program)
  03  Vault                   ← facts_registry.json, job_registry.json (the durable state)
  04  Validation              ← 17 docs + 3 sub-dirs (the operator's desk, the audit trail)
  99  Archive                 ← (frozen snapshots; not actively populated)

  The boundary test (test_00_99_boundary.py) enforces that code in 02_Technical/ cannot import from 03_Vault/ or
  04_Validation/, and that tests cannot import from src/ (with the single whitelisted from src.server.app import app).

  ———

  ## 8. The 4-gate pipeline (what the audit does)

  .txt / .docx / .pdf
     │
     ▼ extractors.py (utf-8 / python-docx / pypdf)
     │
     ▼ Gate 1: Deception scanner (engines/deception_scanner.py)
     │   - 54-pattern ontology v3.9 (engines/deception_ontology_data.py)
     │   - Shannon entropy (>4.5 = anomaly, <2.5 = manipulatively coherent)
     │   - 0.6·avg_confidence + 0.2·coverage + 0.2·anomaly → probability
     │   - verdict: CLEAN / FLAGGED / SUPPRESSED
     │   - if SUPPRESSED: Squeal Protocol writes JSON to 04_Validation/squeal-reports/
     │
     ▼ extract_product_evidence (io/evidence_parser.py) -- regex parser
     │   - productName, pricePaid, priceAdvertised, specClaimed, specMeasured,
     │     warrantyMonths, monthsToFailure, knownIssues, totalFeaturesOrParts,
     │     regulatoryRequirements, violationsFound
     │
     ▼ Gate 2: BBFB engine (engines/bbfb_engine.py)
     │   - 5 ratios (price / spec / warranty / issue / violation)
     │   - LAW: 6 multiplicative gates (one fails = whole product fails)
     │   - GRACE: 2.0·(p_fail² + d_tech² + g_comp²) -- quadratic penalty
     │   - FRUIT: 0.4·cost + 0.3·performance + 0.2·reliability + 0.1·compliance
     │   - CVS = LAW·(FRUIT - Penalty_GRACE), threshold 0.0005
     │
     ▼ Gate 3: Real-options lattice (engines/real_options_lattice.py)
     │   - Cox-Ross-Rubinstein compound binomial (S0=55, K1=18, K2=10, T1=T2=3,
     │     r=0.05, σ1=0.30, σ2=0.20, n1=n2=3, learning_delta=10, striking_ratio=0.85)
     │   - volatility adjusts from deception_score + entropy
     │   - decision: GO if V_total > 0.85·(K1+K2) else DEFER
     │
     ▼ Gate 4: Decision (agents/orchestrator.py)
     │   - REFUSED if any CRITICAL pattern (orchestrator refuses the cycle)
     │   - REVIEW_REQUIRED if any HIGH pattern
     │   - REJECT if BBFB non-compliant
     │   - TEST FIRST if lattice DEFER
     │   - GO otherwise
     │
     ▼ Seal to Merkle chain (io/vault_io.py)
     │   - current_hash = SHA256(previous_hash + canonical_json({event, payload, ts}))
     │   - nizk_proof = SHA256(canonical_json(payload) + "|" + "Justin Barnett")
     │
     ▼ output: Markdown / PDF / DOCX report + Section 177 affidavit + Section 56 ACL demand

  Every gate has a file:line in 04_Validation/AUDIT_NO_BLACK_BOX.md. Every number on the screen has a function behind
  it. There is no model server, no embedding, no LLM, no third-party API in this path.

  ———

  ## 9. The 5 agents + the orchestrator

  Form_Entry_Agent       URN OGIR:02:FORM_ENTRY        intake + research + normalize
  Audit_Review_Agent     URN OGIR:02:AUDIT_TEXT        54-pattern scan + 3-state verdict
  Lattice_Compute_Agent  URN OGIR:01:COMPUTE_LATTICE  real-options binomial valuation
  Ledger_Seal_Agent      URN OGIR:03:SEAL_FACT         Merkle seal via vault_io
  Affidavit_Agent        URN OGIR:04:COMPILE_AFF      Section 177 certificate

  + MonitorAgent         (human-in-the-loop oversight; re-derives chain, scans for hide-patterns, emits Incident
  Briefing)
  + DiscoveryAgent       (forward-environment reconnaissance; 14 sections, deterministic report with SHA-256
  fingerprint)
  + InventoryAgent       (drive inventory; 9-tag classifier, streaming SHA-256, redacts credentials from chain)
  + TauFirewall          (10% extraction ceiling enforcement; thread-safe)
  + JobDelegator         (MCP hand-off; URN OGIR:<SPACE>:<ACTION>, SHA-256-of-canonical-JSON job tokens)

  Orchestrator: the single runtime entry point. Creates a job token per step, claims it, runs the work, closes it. Every
  step is sealed to the chain.

  ———

  ## 11. The IP rights (in one sentence)

  The operator (Justin Barnett) is the original author of every line of source, every constant, every agent, every
  engine, every section of the operator manual, every sealed block on the Merkle chain. Any person may read, run, audit,
  copy the backup plan, extend the build with new agents, rebrand it, and redistribute the source, subject to
  attribution, integrity, audit-trail, and jurisdiction reservations. The jurisdiction is the Commonwealth of Australia.
  The chain is the source of truth. (Full text: 04_Validation/INTELLECTUAL_PROPERTY_RIGHTS.txt.)

  ———

  ### 11. Bump requirements.txt to fastapi>=0.118.3 and pydantic>=2.12.0 (MEDIUM, 5 min, next session)

  The current 02_Technical/requirements.txt has fastapi>=0.115.0 and pydantic>=2.8.0. Only FastAPI 0.118.3+ (released
  2025-10-10) and pydantic 2.12.0+ explicitly support Python 3.14. A fresh pip install on a clean host with Python
  3.14.6 will resolve fastapi>=0.115.0 to 0.115.x (which has no official 3.14 support and no CI matrix entry for it) and
  pydantic>=2.8.0 to 2.10.x or 2.11.x (which pre-dates the 2.12.0 "initial Python 3.14 support" milestone).

  Action: open 02_Technical/requirements.txt, change fastapi>=0.115.0 to fastapi>=0.118.3 and pydantic>=2.8.0 to
  pydantic>=2.12.0. Run python -m pytest tests/ to confirm nothing breaks. Pin CPython to 3.14.5+ (3.14.0-3.14.4 had a
  GC regression reverted in 3.14.5; the codebase is fine on 3.14.6 already). Seal REQUIREMENTS_BUMP_2026_xx_xx with the
  old and new version strings in the payload. Time: 5 minutes. Severity: HIGH per the research doc (a fresh install on a

  ### 12. Tighten SHANNON_ANOMALY_THRESHOLD from 4.5 to 4.0 (MEDIUM, 10 min, next session)

  The current value in 02_Technical/config/constants.py:73 is SHANNON_ANOMALY_THRESHOLD = 4.5. Published 2024-2026 work
  (Thorat & Yang arXiv:2410.14875; SILTD Yang et al.; GPT-who NAACL 2024) shows that raw character-level Shannon entropy
  is a poor LLM-detection feature, but where it does separate populations, the boundary is closer to 3.5-4.0 for English
  text and 4.0-4.5 for short social-media posts. Setting the threshold at 4.5 means the runtime only flags text that is
  essentially non-human (encrypted, base64, machine-emitted, random). Borderline LLM-generated text (the cases the
  operator is most likely auditing) sits in the 3.5-4.5 band and passes through.

  Action: open 02_Technical/config/constants.py, change SHANNON_ANOMALY_THRESHOLD = 4.5 to SHANNON_ANOMALY_THRESHOLD =
  4.0. Update 00_Strategy/GOVERNANCE.md clause 7 ("Shannon Entropy" — the doc says H > 4.5 is anomalous) to match.
  Update 01_Methodology/MATHEMATICS.md to match. Run python -m pytest tests/ — the 8-case evaluation suite may show
  different verdicts (EVAL-002 and EVAL-005 may go from CLEAN to FLAGGED; verify the expected outcomes are still
  correct). Update 04_Validation/STAGE_PAPER_WEEKLY.txt step 3 and STAGE_PAPER_MONTHLY.txt step 2 if they reference the
  threshold. Seal CONSTANTS_BUMP_SHANNON_2026_xx_xx with the old and new values + the test result. Time: 10 minutes (5
  to find all references, 5 to update + test + seal). Severity: HIGH per the research doc (in production now; borderline
  LLM text passes through).

  ### 13. Tighten DECEPTION_PROBABILITY_VETO and SQUEAL_TRIGGER_PROBABILITY from 0.75 to 0.65 (MEDIUM, 10 min, next
  session)

  The current values in 02_Technical/config/constants.py:80,100 are DECEPTION_PROBABILITY_VETO = 0.75 and
  SQUEAL_TRIGGER_PROBABILITY = 0.75. 2024-2026 detection literature (Anthropic 2024 scheming detection, Apollo Research
  2024) shows the "obvious deception" boundary (scam messages, deepfake advertisements) is detectable at >0.90 by simple
  heuristics, while the "subtle deception" band (persuasive-but-misleading, partially true claims) sits at 0.50-0.75.
  Setting the veto at 0.75 catches the obvious case but lets the subtle case through. The Squeal Protocol is intended
  for forensic / audit-trail purposes — the 0.75 threshold is conservative because the cost of a false-positive
  (operator investigates a legitimate message) is low, and the cost of a false-negative (a real deception goes
  unrecorded) is high. The Squeal log is already designed to be high-volume (the operator reviews it), so the marginal
  cost of additional entries is low.

  Action: open 02_Technical/config/constants.py, change both values from 0.75 to 0.65. Update 00_Strategy/GOVERNANCE.md
  clause 7 ("Structural Deception Flag" — the doc says probability > 0.75). Update 01_Methodology/MATHEMATICS.md if it
  references the value. Update 02_Technical/04_Validation/hardcopy/OPERATOR_MANUAL.txt "CONSTANTS" section
  (DECEPTION_PROBABILITY_VETO = 0.75 → 0.65). Run the 8-case evaluation suite — EVAL-005 (False Certainty, expected
  probability 0.3-0.6) may now pass the veto and trigger the Squeal Protocol; that is the desired behaviour. Seal
  CONSTANTS_BUMP_DECEPTION_VETO_2026_xx_xx with the old and new values + the test result. Time: 10 minutes. Severity:
  MEDIUM.

  ### 14. Add canonical-JSON _canonical_default() for datetime/UUID/Decimal/Enum/Path (HIGH, 1 hour, next session)

  The current 02_Technical/src/io/vault_io.py:130 and 02_Technical/src/verify_chain.py:39-47 do json.dumps(payload,
  sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":")) with no default= callable. Any non-JSON-native type (datetime, UUID, Decimal,
  set, custom object) in the payload raises TypeError: Object of type XXX is not JSON serializable and breaks the Merkle
  chain (no block sealed, no error handler in the caller). This is a latent foot-gun — today the fact dict is built by
  hand at facts_registry.py:44-52 and is JSON-safe, so this has not surfaced. But the moment someone adds a createdAt:
  datetime to the payload, the chain breaks. Worse: the recommended default=str band-aid is itself a foot-gun — Python's
  str(datetime.now(timezone.utc)) returns "2026-07-12 14:33:21.123456+00:00" (space separator, no Z suffix), which is
  NOT the ISO-8601 Z form the project uses elsewhere. A REPL-built payload would hash differently from a vault-built
  payload, breaking the Merkle root.

  Action: add a shared _canonical_default() callable in a new module 02_Technical/src/utils/canonical.py (or in
  vault_io.py itself). It must emit:

  - datetime as .isoformat() with Z suffix (not Python's str())
  - UUID as str(uuid)
  - Decimal as str(decimal) (or float(decimal) if you want numeric — but string is safer for hash stability)
  - Enum as .value
  - Path as str(path)
  - set / frozenset as sorted(s) (NOT list(s) — sorting is required for deterministic hashing)
  - NaN / Inf → raise ValueError (do not let them through; allow_nan=False is unreliable on Python 3.10-3.14 per the
    research doc)

  - -0.0 → 0.0 (collapse, to prevent two systems doing the same arithmetic from getting different hashes)
  - All string values and keys must be passed through unicodedata.normalize("NFC", s) to prevent NFC/NFD drift (matters
    on macOS HFS+, iOS String bridging, many web sources)

  Then apply _canonical_default=... to every json.dumps(...) call in vault_io.py and verify_chain.py. Add a unit test
  that constructs a payload with datetime.now(timezone.utc), uuid.uuid4(), Decimal("599.00"), Path("foo"), {"a", "b"}
  and asserts the hash is deterministic across two consecutive runs. Seal CANONICAL_JSON_HARDENED_2026_xx_xx. Time: 1
  hour (30 min to write, 15 min to test, 15 min to seal + update the doc that lists the foot-gun). Severity: HIGH
  (latent; the band-aid is the bug).

  ### 15. Harden deploy.ps1:178-184 against $null .LinkType (LOW, 5 min, next session)

  The current deploy/deploy.ps1:178-184 does $item = Get-Item $InstallPath -Force -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue then if
  ($item.LinkType -in @("SymbolicLink", "Junction")) — on OneDrive Files On-Demand or UNC reparse points, .LinkType may
  be $null and the branch falls through to the "real directory" WARN path, which logs a misleading warning. The fix is 4
  lines:

  # Current (line 178-184)
  $item = Get-Item $InstallPath -Force -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
  if ($item.LinkType -in @("SymbolicLink", "Junction")) {

  # Recommended

  the dry-run report still parses (the existing test_a5_deploy_dry_run.py tests will catch a regression — the three

  Severity: LOW (intermittently reproducible on operator hosts with cloud-only or UNC paths).

  ### 16. Add webviewInstallMode: downloadBootstrapper to tauri.conf.json (LOW, 5 min, next session)

  The current 02_Technical/tauri-shell/tauri.conf.json does not specify webviewInstallMode. Tauri 2 still uses WiX 3.14
  by default for MSI bundling; unsigned MSIs trigger SmartScreen on Win 11 24H2. The webviewInstallMode:
  downloadBootstrapper declaration tells the Tauri installer to download the latest WebView2 runtime at install time,
  which is the right default for Win 10 compat (some Win 10 hosts do not have WebView2 preinstalled).

  Action: open 02_Technical/tauri-shell/tauri.conf.json, add a "webviewInstallMode": "downloadBootstrapper" key under
  the bundle object. Run npx tauri build to confirm the bundle config is valid (the build will fail loudly if the key is
  in the wrong place). Seal TAURI_WEBVIEW_FIXED_2026_xx_xx. Time: 5 minutes (assuming the build env is up; if not, this
  is a longer cycle). Severity: LOW (WiX 3.14 is in maintenance mode, SmartScreen is a UX problem not a security one).

  ### 17. Switch the agentic REPL from tcoxav/aegis to qwen2.5-coder:7b (or qwen3:1.7b) (LOW, 15 min, next session — but
  only when Ollama is stable)

  The current 02_Technical/tools/agentic_repl.py has DEFAULT_MODEL = os.environ.get("OGIR_AGENT_MODEL", "tcoxav/
  aegis:latest") — the 1.5B Qwen2 model. Per the MikeVeerman 2025 tool-calling benchmark, qwen2:1.5b scores 0.80 on tool
  calling, qwen3:1.7b scores 0.96 (winner of the benchmark), and qwen2.5-coder:7b is the operator-resourced 7B Qwen2.5-
  Coder (already available locally, scores ~0.85-0.92 Agent Score). The 1.5B model is the second-best small model;
  switching to a 1.7B or 7B model is a measurable reliability improvement. The operator's decision (sealed 2026-07-12)
  was to use qwen2.5-coder:7b (practical, available locally) over qwen3:1.7b (theoretical best, requires a 1.1 GB
  download).

  Action: open 02_Technical/tools/agentic_repl.py, change DEFAULT_MODEL = ... to qwen2.5-coder:7b (or qwen3:1.7b if you
  prefer the theoretical best). Set "options": {"temperature": 0.1, "seed": 42} on every Ollama request for determinism.
  Set keep_alive: "-1m" on the first request of a session (or set OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=-1 as an env var on the Ollama
  host). Verify with a test prompt: send "What is the weather in Paris?" with the get_weather tool and confirm the
  response contains tool_calls[0].function.name == "get_weather". Then re-run the
  test_d5_agentic_repl.py::test_agentic_repl_end_to_end test (currently skip-guarded) — it should now pass. Seal
  AGENTIC_REPL_MODEL_UPGRADE_2026_xx_xx. Time: 15 minutes (assuming Ollama + the new model are already pulled; if not,
  add 5-10 minutes for ollama pull). Severity: LOW (the current model works; this is a quality improvement).

  ## What I will do if you say "do #14"

  Five steps, in this order, with the chain sealed at the end:

  1. Open 02_Technical/src/io/vault_io.py and 02_Technical/src/verify_chain.py. Read every json.dumps(...) call. There
     are 4 in vault_io.py (in _atomic_write_json, append_block, _canonical_json, job_append) and 1 in verify_chain.py
     (in _canonical_json).

  2. Add a new module 02_Technical/src/utils/canonical.py containing a _canonical_default(obj) callable. It will handle
     datetime (.isoformat() with Z), UUID (str(uuid)), Decimal (str(decimal)), Enum (.value), Path (str(path)), set /
     frozenset (sorted(s)), NaN / Inf (raise ValueError), -0.0 (collapse to 0.0). It will also pass every string value
     and key through unicodedata.normalize("NFC", s) to prevent NFC/NFD drift. It will raise TypeError for anything not
     in the allow-list (this is the opposite of the default=str band-aid — explicit failure, not silent corruption).

  3. Replace every json.dumps(payload, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":")) call with json.dumps(payload,
     sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), default=_canonical_default). The new default= callable will be imported from
     src.utils.canonical (new) or defined locally in vault_io.py (smaller diff). Either is fine; the test enforces the
     behaviour.

  4. Add a new test tests/test_canonical_json_hardening.py that builds a payload containing a
     datetime.now(timezone.utc), a uuid.uuid4(), a Decimal("599.00"), a Path("foo"), a {"a", "b"} set, and a
     frozenset({"x"}); hashes it twice; asserts the two hashes are byte-identical; asserts "-0.0" is not in the output
     (the -0.0 collapse); asserts the datetime string ends with "Z" not "+00:00"; asserts NFC normalization works
     ("café" with NFD composes to "café" with NFC). This test will be part of the 51-test suite and will run on every
     future cycle.

  5. Run the full test suite (python -m pytest tests/ -v). Expected: 51 passed, 1 skipped (the Ollama skip), 0 failed.
     Then verify the chain (python -m src.verify_chain). Expected: MATCH, block count one more than 3,102 (because the
     test added 0 blocks; the seal will add 1, so 3,103). Then seal a CANONICAL_JSON_HARDENED_2026_07_16 block to the
     chain with the old and new function signatures in the payload, the test results, and a reference to the new test
     file. Then append a type: "change" entry to 04_Validation/changelog.log.

  Total time: 1 hour. Total risk: a code change in two files and one new test file. Total upside: the foot-gun that the
  RESEARCH_COMPATIBILITY_2026-07-12.md flagged as the only HIGH-severity latent crash in the project is closed, with a
  test that proves it stays closed.

     datetime serialises with Z suffix not +00:00; an unknown type raises TypeError. The test goes through the HTTP

  What this is. The hard-copy backup plan (04_Validation/hardcopy/HARD_COPY_BACKUP_PLAN_1-2-3.txt) promises the build

  burn works, but it does not prove the build runs on a host the operator has never touched.

  What you need to do (2-4 hours, requires a second Windows host):

  Step 1: Find a second Windows host. Options, in order of preference:

  - A family member's Windows laptop (borrow it for 2 hours)
  - A library's Windows PC (most Australian public libraries have Windows 10 or 11)
  - A Windows 11 VM on the MSI Prestige 16 itself (Hyper-V is built into Windows 11 Pro; create a VM with no network, 4
    GB RAM, 50 GB disk, install Windows 11 from a USB stick — total time ~45 min including Windows install, but you
    already have the SDXC usable)

  - A Windows 11 VM on a different laptop if you have one

  The second host MUST be a Windows host the project has never touched. "Different drive letter on the same MSI" does
  not count.

  Step 2: Prepare the USB stick. Take the SDXC at D:\OrderGetItRight\ (or burn a fresh USB from the MSI if the SDXC is
  busy). The USB must contain:

  - The entire OrderGetItRight\ folder from D:\OrderGetItRight\ (Copy B, 3,102 blocks, root 7c348a24...)
  - A paper note with the Merkle root printed on it: 7c348a24d6fd6c3395aee9b7eba2ab89b15e49ec98bd255bedc11f6dfe160504,
    3,102 blocks

  - A one-line instruction sheet: "Open PowerShell. cd D:\OrderGetItRight\02_Technical. python -m src.verify_chain.
    Confirm the printed root matches. Then cd D:\OrderGetItRight && python -m pytest tests/ -v. Confirm 50 pass + 1
    skip."

  Step 3: Boot the second host from the USB. Plug the USB into the second host. Open File Explorer, navigate to D:
  \OrderGetItRight\. If the second host does not have Python 3.12+, install it (Python 3.14.6 from python.org is the
  tested version; the project is forward-compatible to 3.12 per STRATEGY.md).

  Step 4: Run the chain verification. Open PowerShell. cd D:\OrderGetItRight\02_Technical. python -m src.verify_chain.
  Expected output: Claimed root = Recomputed root = 7c348a24d6fd6c3395aee9b7eba2ab89b15e49ec98bd255bedc11f6dfe160504,
  Block count: 3102, RESULT: MATCH. If the printed root on the paper matches and the output matches, the chain is intact
  on this host. If they don't match, the USB is corrupt; re-burn from the MSI and try again.

  Step 5: Run the test suite. cd D:\OrderGetItRight. python -m pytest tests/ -v. Expected: 50 passed, 1 skipped (the
  Ollama tool-calling skip), 0 failed. The 1 skip is normal — the Ollama tool-calling test is skip-guarded and fires
  when no tool-capable model is loaded. On a fresh host with no Ollama, this is the expected behaviour. If the count is
  different, record the exact failure. A real regression here means the build is not portable and there is a real bug to
  find.

  Step 6: Run the no-network audit. python 04_Validation/scripts/audit_no_network.py. Expected: RESULT: PASS -- 0
  network imports in runtime source tree. If the count is non-zero, the runtime has a network import the source-only
  check missed.

  Step 7: Record the result. Three possible outcomes:

  (a) All three pass. Seal in Copy B on the MSI: python -m src.third_party_assistant then seal
  D1_CLEAN_HOST_VERIFIED_2026_xx_xx {"operator": "Justin Barnett", "host": "<make + model of the second host>",
  "host_os": "<Windows 11 23H2 / Windows 10 22H2 / etc.>", "python_version": "<3.14.6>", "chain_root": "7c348a24...",
  "block_count": 3102, "tests": "50 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed", "no_network_audit": "PASS", "verified_at": "<today's
  UTC ISO 8601>"}. The D1 item is closed. The 1-2-3 backup plan is proven.

  (b) Tests fail but chain verifies. This is a real finding. The build is portable at the chain layer but not at the
  test layer. Record the failing test names, the error messages, the host + Python version, and seal
  D1_CLEAN_HOST_PARTIAL_2026_xx_xx with the details. The fix is whatever the failing test is reporting — probably a path
  assumption (Windows vs Unix path separator), a missing dependency that the SDXC re-burn didn't include, or a Python
  version difference.

  (c) Chain fails to verify. This is the worst case. The USB is corrupt, the re-burn missed something, or the on-disk
  JSON has a different encoding on the second host. Re-burn from the MSI, try again. If it still fails, the second host
  has a hardware issue (disk, USB port, RAM) and the test is not a build test, it is a hardware test.

  What you need to buy / transfer / schedule. A second Windows host for 2-4 hours. No money, no transfer, just time. The
  schedule is: pick a Saturday morning, borrow a laptop, do the 7 steps.

  Why I cannot do this for you. I do not have a second host in this room. I cannot insert a USB stick into a laptop that
  is not present. The test is hardware-required.

  2-4 hour commitment, not a 16-hour one.

  ## What I got wrong

  I have been asking you to choose between options, as if you were the technical authority on the project. You are not.
  You are the operator. You made it explicit: the machine does the math and the matching. You give the permission, the
  money, the direction. You do not pick the technical path.

  That means every question I framed as "which do you want to do first, #1 #2 #14" was the wrong question. I was
  treating you as the engineer. You are the operator.

  The correct framing, for every one of the 17 open items, is:

  - Items I can do correctly with the project as it is → I do them, and I tell you what I did.
  - Items I can do but only with your data, your hardware, or a one-time operator action → I tell you exactly what
    physical thing you need to do, in what order, with what tools, and you do it. No decision on your part except "yes,

    you to point me at the original material you put in (the Sovereign Node / Grok transcripts, the LDDE-ZeroTouch doc,
    the Onyx / Cloud / Sovereign Node 9010 lineage) so I can derive the answer from the project's own design history
    rather than guess.

  The 17 items I just gave you were 90% in the first bucket and 10% in the third. I should have been doing them, not
  asking you to rank them. Asking you to rank them was wrong.

  The CONTEXT_WINDOW.md (section 2) lists the live state as: 2,797 blocks, root 3c33d6a18eee0c59.... The live state I
  just verified in Copy B is 3,102 blocks, root 7c348a24.... The CONTEXT_WINDOW's "live state" was the value at the
  moment it was sealed (block 2797, 2026-07-15T22:14:17Z, FORK_RESOLVED_2026_07_16). The chain has grown since.

  The TAURI_BINARY_INVESTIGATION_2026-07-16.md names Copy A (.claude/OrderGetItRight) and Copy B (My Project/
  OrderGetItRight) and says: "The build was not 'lost' — it was built in a different working copy of the project. There

  ### Question B: What does the project say about the canonical-JSON foot-gun?

  The project's own design history is the LDDE-ZeroTouch doc, the Onyx Cloud doc, the Sovereign Node 9010 docs, and the
  RESEARCH_COMPATIBILITY_2026-07-12.md. The latter is in the project, sealed at 2026-07-12, and explicitly identifies
  the default=str band-aid as itself a foot-gun (area 3, "Merkle canonical-JSON foot-guns," severity RED). The doc says:
  "the right fix is a shared _canonical_default() that emits datetime as .isoformat() with Z, UUID as str(uuid), Decimal
  as str(decimal), Enum as .value, Path as str(path), set/frozenset as sorted(s), with explicit NaN/Inf raise and -0.0
  collapse, plus an NFC-normalisation walker."

  The doc is the project's own instruction manual for the fix. I should derive the patch from the doc, not from my own
  design. The test names, the allow-list, the NFC walker, the -0.0 collapse, the strict-fail-on-unknown — all of it is
  in the doc. I am executing the project's own recipe, not inventing a fix.
